**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies. 
We notice that Testimony 3 states Ms. Crane was murdered, and with her dying breath, she managed to retaliate. However, Evidence 11 (Autopsy Report) indicates that the head injury was sustained post-mortem, meaning after death. This implies that Ms. Crane could not have retaliated with her dying breath as she was already dead when the head injury occurred.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction. 
Proposition 9 states that if a person is at a location and moves to another location, then the person is no longer at the first location. While this proposition does deal with movement and location, it does not directly apply to the contradiction between the testimony of retaliation and the post-mortem injury.

**Step 3:** Since none of the listed propositions directly apply to the temporal and causal nature of this contradiction, we proceed to explain the spatial reasoning used. 
The contradiction arises from the timeline of events. The testimony suggests an action (retaliation) occurred at the moment of death, but the evidence (autopsy report) indicates that a significant injury (head injury) happened after death, which contradicts the possibility of any action, including retaliation, at the moment of death.

**Step 4:** Provide the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction. 
The reasoning here is based on the understanding of temporal sequences and the implications of post-mortem injuries. If an injury is post-mortem, it means the person was already dead, and thus, any testimony suggesting action after the point of death contradicts the medical evidence.

- A list of characters, evidences and testimonies
- A set of spatial propositions for analysis

Prop 2: If an object is behind a wall, then the object is not visible from the other side of the wall.
Prop 3: If a person is in front of a door and the door is closed, then the person cannot see what is on the other side of the door.

Prop 7: If a person is facing a wall, then the person cannot see what is behind the wall.
Prop 8: If a door is between two rooms, then a person in one room cannot see into the other room if the door is closed.
Prop 9: If an object is on top of another object, then the first object is above the second object.

Prop 5: If an object is on the floor and a person is standing near it, then the person can potentially see the object.
Prop 6: If a room has only one door and a person is inside the room, then the person cannot leave the room without passing through the door.
Prop 7: If a person is behind a wall and another person is on the other side of the wall, then the two people are separated by the wall.
Prop 8: If a person is in a car and the car crashes, then the person is likely to be injured or killed.
Prop 9: If a person is holding a weapon and a bullet is found near that person, then the person may have fired the weapon.
Prop 10: If a person is in a location and a photograph is taken of that location, then the person can potentially be seen in the photograph.

- A set of spatial propositions for analysis

## SPATIAL PROPOSITIONS:
Prop 1: If a person is in a room and there is a wall between the person and another location, then the person cannot see the other location.
Prop 2: If an object is inside another object, then the inner object is contained by the outer object.
Prop 3: If a person is behind a wall, then the person is not in the same room as a person on the other side of the wall.
Prop 4: If a door is locked, then a person cannot pass through the door.
Prop 5: If a person is in front of a door and the door is open, then the person can pass through the door.
Prop 6: If a person is above a location and there is no elevator or stairs, then the person cannot move to the location.
Prop 7: If a person is on one side of a door and another person is on the other side, then the two persons are separated by the door.
Prop 8: If an object is on top of another object, then the top object is above the bottom object.
Prop 9: If a person is inside a room and the room has only one door, then the person must use the door to leave the room.
Prop 10: If a person is at a location and there is no path to another location, then the person cannot move to the other location.

**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies. 
We notice that Testimony 0 states a photo was recorded 10–20 minutes after the president went up to the roof with a woman, and Testimony 1 indicates that the woman came back down on the elevator alone. However, Evidence 10 (Security Camera Footage) shows President Wang and Judge Gavèlle arriving on the roof but does not show the president riding the elevator down to leave. This implies there might be a contradiction regarding how the president left the roof or if he left at all.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction. 
Proposition 1 (If a person is in a room and there is a wall between the person and another location, then the person cannot see the other location) does not directly apply here since the issue is about movement and visibility in terms of elevator usage, not walls. Proposition 5 (If a person is in front of a door and the door is open, then the person can pass through the door) and Proposition 9 (If a person is inside a room and the room has only one door, then the person must use the door to leave the room) are relevant because they deal with the necessity of using a door (or in this case, an elevator) to move between areas.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction. 
Proposition 9 is particularly relevant because it implies that if the president was on the roof (considered a "room" in this context) and there's only one way down (the elevator), then he must have used the elevator to leave, unless there's an alternative method of descent not accounted for by the testimonies or evidences provided. However, Testimony 3 suggests there's no other way for the president to get down without using the elevator, which directly conflicts with the lack of evidence showing the president using the elevator to descend, as per Evidence 10.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction. 
Given the context, spatial reasoning indicates a discrepancy between the expected behavior (the president using the elevator to leave the roof) and the observed behavior (no record of the president using the elevator to leave). This discrepancy suggests a contradiction between the testimonies implying the president's descent should have been recorded and the evidence showing no such record exists.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format. 
Given the analysis, the contradiction lies between Evidence 10 (Security Camera Footage) and Testimony 3 (There's no other way for the president to get down without using the elevator, is there?), as they create a conflict regarding the president's method of leaving the roof.

{"evidence": 10, "testimony": 3}

  "testimony": 3

Prop 1: If a person is in a room and the door is locked, then the person cannot exit the room through the door.
Prop 2: If an object is inside another object, then the inner object is contained by the outer object.
Prop 3: If a person is wearing a costume, then the costume covers the person's body.
Prop 4: If a wall separates two locations, then a person in one location cannot see a person in the other location.
Prop 5: If a person is shot with a weapon, then the person is in the same location as the weapon.
Prop 6: If a door is jammed from the inside, then a person outside the door cannot enter the room.
Prop 7: If a person is blindfolded, then the person cannot see their surroundings.
Prop 8: If a person is cuffed, then the person's hands are restrained.
Prop 9: If a passageway connects two locations, then a person can travel from one location to the other through the passageway.
Prop 10: If a person is behind a wall, then the person is not in the same room as a person on the other side of the wall.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction.
Proposition 1 states that if a person is in a room and the door is locked, then the person cannot exit the room through the door. This seems relevant because Lance testifies that the door leading outside was locked tight, which aligns with the condition of this proposition.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction.
However, upon closer inspection, the direct contradiction isn't about the locked door itself but rather about the circumstances of Lance's escape and the state of the room or hideout. The critical piece of evidence is the Broken Prop Sword (Evidence 12), which was used to jam the handle to the hideout's door from the inside. This implies that the door was not just locked but also jammed from the inside, which aligns with Proposition 6 (If a door is jammed from the inside, then a person outside the door cannot enter the room). The contradiction arises when considering Lance's testimony about escaping through the underground passageway because the door was locked (Testimony 5) and the fact that he was hit with the Broken Prop Sword, which was used to jam the door, suggesting he might have been involved in the jamming or was at least present when it happened.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction.
The spatial reasoning here involves understanding the layout and accessibility of the hideout, including how doors and passageways function in relation to the characters' movements. The contradiction lies in the details of Lance's escape and the condition of the door, which, when jammed from the inside, would prevent outsiders from entering but doesn't directly address how Lance escaped, given his testimonies about being cuffed, blindfolded, and the door being locked.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format, focusing on the specific evidence and testimony that highlight the contradiction based on the spatial analysis.
Given the analysis, the contradiction is best highlighted by Evidence 12 (Broken Prop Sword) and Testimony 3 (My hands were cuffed, but it was a stroke of luck that they left me alone), as these two elements raise questions about Lance's ability to escape given his claimed state (cuffed and presumably unable to manipulate objects like the prop sword to jam a door) and the physical state of the hideout's door.
